## H.3Lower bound for Theorem6

## Proof of the lower bound in
Theorem6.

We treat the three noise regimes separately.

## Low noise

Leth=hnh=h_{n}, and choose a nonzeroψ∈Cc∞​(B​(0,1))\psi\in C_{c}^{\infty}(B(0,1))satisfying∫ψ=0\int\psi=0. For0≤u≤10\leq u\leq 1, putψu=ψ∗φu(d)\psi_{u}=\psi*\varphi_{u}^{(d)}. By Plancherel’s
identity,‖∇ψu‖22=1(2​π)d​∫ℝd‖ξ‖2​e−u​‖ξ‖2​|ψ^​(ξ)|2​dξ>0.\|\nabla\psi_{u}\|_{2}^{2}=\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\|\xi\|^{2}e^{-u\|\xi\|^{2}}|\widehat{\psi}(\xi)|^{2}\mathrm{d}\xi>0.

Continuity inuu, compactness of[0,1][0,1], and uniform Gaussian decay
therefore give a fixedA>1A>1such thatinf0≤u≤1∫‖z‖≤A‖∇ψu​(z)‖2​dz≳1,sup0≤u≤1∫‖z‖≤A|ψu​(z)|2​dz≲1.\inf_{0\leq u\leq 1}\int_{\|z\|\leq A}\|\nabla\psi_{u}(z)\|^{2}\mathrm{d}z\gtrsim 1,\qquad\sup_{0\leq u\leq 1}\int_{\|z\|\leq A}|\psi_{u}(z)|^{2}\mathrm{d}z\lesssim 1.(45)

Choosem≍h−dm\asymp h^{-d}pointsx1,…,xm∈[−1/4,1/4]dx_{1},\ldots,x_{m}\in[-1/4,1/4]^{d}, separated by at leastK​hKh, whereK>2​A+2K>2A+2is fixed. Forθ∈{−1,1}m\theta\in\{-1,1\}^{m}, definefθ​(x)=φd​(x)+δ​hα​∑j=1mθj​ψ​(x−xjh),rθ=fθφd,f_{\theta}(x)=\varphi_{d}(x)+\delta h^{\alpha}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\theta_{j}\psi\left(\frac{x-x_{j}}{h}\right),\qquad r_{\theta}=\frac{f_{\theta}}{\varphi_{d}},

whereδ>0\delta>0is a sufficiently small constant. Since the
perturbations have disjoint supports in a fixed compact set and∫ψ=0\int\psi=0, the interior-point assumption impliesfθ∈ℱα,df_{\theta}\in\mathcal{F}_{\alpha,d}for everyθ\theta.

## Proof of Theorem7.

LetXXbe the true reverse processd​Xu=sn−u​(Xu)​d​u+d​Bu,X0∼pn,\mathrm{d}X_{u}=s_{n-u}(X_{u})\mathrm{d}u+\mathrm{d}B_{u},\qquad X_{0}\sim p_{n},

flowHaussmann and Pardoux (1986),Xn∼fX_{n}\sim f. Conditional on𝒟n\mathcal{D}_{n}, letYYsolved​Yu=s~n−u​(Yu)​d​u+d​Bu,Y0=X0,\mathrm{d}Y_{u}=\widetilde{s}_{n-u}(Y_{u})\mathrm{d}u+\mathrm{d}B_{u},\qquad Y_{0}=X_{0},

with the same Brownian motion. This gives a coupling offfandℒ​(Yn∣𝒟n)\mathcal{L}(Y_{n}\mid\mathcal{D}_{n}).

PutΔu=Yu−Xu\Delta_{u}=Y_{u}-X_{u}. The one-sided Lipschitz inequality fors~n−u\widetilde{s}_{n-u}gives, for almost everyuu,dd​u​‖Δu‖≤λd​(n−u)​‖Δu‖+‖s~n−u​(Xu)−sn−u​(Xu)‖.\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}u}\|\Delta_{u}\|\leq\lambda_{d}(n-u)\|\Delta_{u}\|+\|\widetilde{s}_{n-u}(X_{u})-s_{n-u}(X_{u})\|.

Gronwall’s inequality and the change of variablest=n−ut=n-utherefore yield‖Δn‖≤∫0nexp⁡{∫0tλd​(q)​dq}​‖s~t​(Xn−t)−st​(Xn−t)‖​dt.\|\Delta_{n}\|\leq\int_{0}^{n}\exp\left\{\int_{0}^{t}\lambda_{d}(q)\mathrm{d}q\right\}\|\widetilde{s}_{t}(X_{n-t})-s_{t}(X_{n-t})\|\mathrm{d}t.

For0<t≤10<t\leq 1, the integral in the exponential is uniformly bounded. Fort>1t>1,∫1tλd​(q)​dq=K0​log⁡(2​t1+t)−log⁡t.\int_{1}^{t}\lambda_{d}(q)\mathrm{d}q=K_{0}\log\left(\frac{2t}{1+t}\right)-\log t.

Consequently,exp⁡{∫0tλd​(q)​dq}≲11+t,t>0.\exp\left\{\int_{0}^{t}\lambda_{d}(q)\mathrm{d}q\right\}\lesssim\frac{1}{1+t},\qquad t>0.

SinceXn−t∼ptX_{n-t}\sim p_{t}, Minkowski’s inequality and
(51) give{𝔼f​W22​(f,ℒ​(Yn∣𝒟n))}1/2\displaystyle\left\{\mathbb{E}_{f}W_{2}^{2}\bigl(f,\mathcal{L}(Y_{n}\mid\mathcal{D}_{n})\bigr)\right\}^{1/2}≤(𝔼f​‖Δn‖2)1/2≲∫0n11+t​{𝔼f​∫ℝd‖s~t−st‖2​pt}1/2​dt\displaystyle\leq\left(\mathbb{E}_{f}\|\Delta_{n}\|^{2}\right)^{1/2}\lesssim\int_{0}^{n}\frac{1}{1+t}\left\{\mathbb{E}_{f}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\|\widetilde{s}_{t}-s_{t}\|^{2}p_{t}\right\}^{1/2}\mathrm{d}t≲∫0n𝔯n,d​(t)1+t​dt.\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{n}\frac{\sqrt{\mathfrak{r}_{n,d}(t)}}{1+t}\mathrm{d}t.

It remains to replace the initializationpnp_{n}byγn\gamma_{n}. CoupleY0∼pnY_{0}\sim p_{n}andY^0∼γn\widehat{Y}_{0}\sim\gamma_{n}optimally and drive the two
projected reverse diffusions with the same Brownian motion. The same
one-sided Lipschitz calculation givesW2​(ℒ​(Yn∣𝒟n),μ^n)≲11+n​W2​(pn,γn)≤11+n​W2​(f,φd)≲n−1.W_{2}\bigl(\mathcal{L}(Y_{n}\mid\mathcal{D}_{n}),\widehat{\mu}_{n}\bigr)\lesssim\frac{1}{1+n}W_{2}(p_{n},\gamma_{n})\leq\frac{1}{1+n}W_{2}(f,\varphi_{d})\lesssim n^{-1}.

The last bound is uniform overℱα,d\mathcal{F}_{\alpha,d}, sincef≤C​φdf\leq C\varphi_{d}. The triangle inequality and
Lemma18complete the proof.
∎

## Proof of Proposition5.

SinceP,Q∈𝒞λΛP,Q\in{\cal C}_{\lambda}^{\Lambda}, the projection property and the triangle
inequality giveW2​(P,P~n)≤W2​(P,P^n)+W2​(P^n,P~n)≤2​W2​(P,P^n);W2​(Q,Q~n)≤2​W2​(Q,Q^n).W_{2}(P,\widetilde{P}_{n})\leq W_{2}(P,\widehat{P}_{n})+W_{2}(\widehat{P}_{n},\widetilde{P}_{n})\leq 2W_{2}(P,\widehat{P}_{n});\qquad W_{2}(Q,\widetilde{Q}_{n})\leq 2W_{2}(Q,\widehat{Q}_{n}).

SinceP~n,Q~n∈𝒞λΛ\widetilde{P}_{n},\widetilde{Q}_{n}\in{\cal C}_{\lambda}^{\Lambda},
applying Caffarelli’s contraction theoremCaffarelli (2000)toT~n∗\widetilde{T}^{*}_{n}and to its inverse
givesT~n∗=∇φ~n\widetilde{T}^{*}_{n}=\nabla\widetilde{\varphi}_{n}, whose Brenier potentialφ~n\widetilde{\varphi}_{n}has two-sided Hessian bounds: it ismφ​(λ,Λ)m_{\varphi}(\lambda,\Lambda)-strongly convex andMφ​(λ,Λ)M_{\varphi}(\lambda,\Lambda)-smooth.

We now apply Theorem8with reference mapT~n∗\widetilde{T}_{n}^{*}, reference marginals(P~n,Q~n)(\widetilde{P}_{n},\widetilde{Q}_{n}), and perturbed marginals(P,Q)(P,Q). Since
the optimal coupling betweenPPandQQis induced byT∗T^{*}, the theorem
controls∫‖T∗​(x)−T~n∗​(x)‖2​P​(d​x)\int\|T^{*}(x)-\widetilde{T}_{n}^{*}(x)\|^{2}\,P(\mathrm{d}x), and gives𝔼​‖T~n∗−T∗‖L2​(P)2≲mφ,Mφ𝔼​W22​(P,P~n)+𝔼​W22​(Q,Q~n)≲ρn2.\mathbb{E}\|\widetilde{T}^{*}_{n}-T^{*}\|_{L^{2}(P)}^{2}\lesssim_{m_{\varphi},M_{\varphi}}\mathbb{E}W_{2}^{2}(P,\widetilde{P}_{n})+\mathbb{E}W_{2}^{2}(Q,\widetilde{Q}_{n})\lesssim\rho_{n}^{2}.

Substituting the projection bounds above yields the claimedρn2\rho_{n}^{2}rate.

Finally, if the two marginal densities belong toℱα,d\mathcal{F}_{\alpha,d}andP^n,Q^n\widehat{P}_{n},\widehat{Q}_{n}are generated as in
Algorithm2, then
Theorem7givesρn=ϵn,d\rho_{n}=\epsilon_{n,d}, yielding the stated plug-in rate.
∎

## Proof of Theorem12.

We first prove a lower bound onℰ​(P~,Q~)\mathcal{E}(\widetilde{P},\widetilde{Q}).
For a fixedxx, the pointT∗​(x)T^{*}(x)minimizesy↦D​(x,y)y\mapsto D(x,y).
HenceD​(x,T∗​(x))=0D(x,T^{*}(x))=0,∇yD​(x,T∗​(x))=0\nabla_{y}D(x,T^{*}(x))=0.
By the assumption∇y​y2D​(x,y)⪰μy​I\nabla^{2}_{yy}D(x,y)\succeq\mu_{y}I, Taylor’s theorem givesD​(x,y)≥μy​‖y−T∗​(x)‖22.D(x,y)\geq\mu_{y}\frac{\|y-T^{*}(x)\|^{2}}{2}.

Thereforeℰ​(P~,Q~)=∫D​(x,y)​dπ~​(x,y)≥μy​∫‖y−T∗​(x)‖22​dπ~​(x,y).\mathcal{E}(\widetilde{P},\widetilde{Q})=\int D(x,y)\,\mathrm{d}\widetilde{\pi}(x,y)\geq\mu_{y}\int\frac{\|y-T^{*}(x)\|^{2}}{2}\,\mathrm{d}\widetilde{\pi}(x,y).

It remains to upper boundℰ​(P~,Q~)\mathcal{E}(\widetilde{P},\widetilde{Q}).
LetγP∈Π​(P,P~)\gamma_{P}\in\Pi(P,\widetilde{P})be optimal forW2​(P,P~)W_{2}(P,\widetilde{P}), and letγQ∈Π​(Q,Q~)\gamma_{Q}\in\Pi(Q,\widetilde{Q})be optimal forW2​(Q,Q~)W_{2}(Q,\widetilde{Q}). Also defineπ∗:=(id,T∗)#​P∈Π​(P,Q)\pi^{*}:=(\mathrm{id},T^{*})_{\#}P\in\Pi(P,Q).
SinceγP\gamma_{P}andπ∗\pi^{*}have the same first marginalPP, the
gluing lemma gives a probability measure on triples(X,U,Y)(X,U,Y)such that(X,U)∼γP(X,U)\sim\gamma_{P},(X,Y)∼π∗(X,Y)\sim\pi^{*}.
In particular,Y=T∗​(X)Y=T^{*}(X)almost surely. SinceY∼QY\sim Q, and since the
first marginal ofγQ\gamma_{Q}is alsoQQ, another application of the gluing
lemma gives a joint distribution of(X,U,Y,V)(X,U,Y,V)such that(X,U)∼γP(X,U)\sim\gamma_{P},(X,Y)∼π∗(X,Y)\sim\pi^{*},(Y,V)∼γQ(Y,V)\sim\gamma_{Q}.
Consequently,(U,V)∈Π​(P~,Q~)(U,V)\in\Pi(\widetilde{P},\widetilde{Q}). ThereforeWc​(P~,Q~)≤𝔼​c​(V−U)W_{c}(\widetilde{P},\widetilde{Q})\leq\mathbb{E}c(V-U).
Using the marginals ofUUandVV, we obtainℰ​(P~,Q~)≤𝔼​c​(V−U)−𝔼​φ​(U)−𝔼​ψ​(V)=𝔼​D​(U,V)\mathcal{E}(\widetilde{P},\widetilde{Q})\leq\mathbb{E}c(V-U)-\mathbb{E}\varphi(U)-\mathbb{E}\psi(V)=\mathbb{E}D(U,V).

NowD≥0D\geq 0everywhere, whileD​(X,Y)=D​(X,T∗​(X))=0D(X,Y)=D(X,T^{*}(X))=0.
Thus(X,Y)(X,Y)is a global minimizer ofDD, and hence∇xD​(X,Y)=0\nabla_{x}D(X,Y)=0,∇yD​(X,Y)=0\nabla_{y}D(X,Y)=0almost surely.
Taylor’s theorem, together with the assumed Hessian bounds as well as‖∇x​y2D​(x,y)‖op=‖∇2c​(y−x)‖op≤Lc\|\nabla^{2}_{xy}D(x,y)\|_{\mathrm{op}}=\|\nabla^{2}c(y-x)\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq L_{c}, gives𝔼​D​(U,V)≤Lx​𝔼​‖U−X‖22+Ly​𝔼​‖V−Y‖22+Lc​𝔼​[‖U−X‖​‖V−Y‖].\mathbb{E}D(U,V)\leq L_{x}\mathbb{E}\frac{\|U-X\|^{2}}{2}+L_{y}\mathbb{E}\frac{\|V-Y\|^{2}}{2}+L_{c}\mathbb{E}\big[\|U-X\|\|V-Y\|\big].

## Proof of Corollary3.

Apply Theorem12with(P~n,Q~n)(\widetilde{P}_{n},\widetilde{Q}_{n})as the reference pair and(P,Q)(P,Q)as the
perturbed pair. The optimal coupling betweenPPandQQis induced byTcT^{c}, so the left-hand side in the theorem becomes the displayedL2​(P)L^{2}(P)error. The two Wasserstein perturbation terms are both bounded byρn\rho_{n}, which gives the claim.
∎
